    59	func (vm *VM) Run(program *Program, env any) (_ any, err error) {
    60		defer func() {
    61			if r := recover(); r != nil {
    62				var location file.Location
    63				if vm.ip-1 < len(program.locations) {
    64					location = program.locations[vm.ip-1]
    65				}
    66				f := &file.Error{
    67					Location: location,
    68					Message:  fmt.Sprintf("%v", r),
    69				}
    70				if err, ok := r.(error); ok {
    71					f.Wrap(err)
    72				}
    73				err = f.Bind(program.source)
    74			}
    75		}()
    76	
    77		if vm.Stack == nil {
    78			vm.Stack = make([]any, 0, 2)
    79		} else {
    80			clearSlice(vm.Stack)
    81			vm.Stack = vm.Stack[0:0]
    82		}
    83		if vm.Scopes != nil {
    84			clearSlice(vm.Scopes)
    85			vm.Scopes = vm.Scopes[0:0]
    86		}
    87		vm.scopePoolIdx = 0 // Reset pool index for reuse
    88		vm.currScope = nil
    89		if len(vm.Variables) < program.variables {
    90			vm.Variables = make([]any, program.variables)
    91		}
    92		if vm.MemoryBudget == 0 {
    93			vm.MemoryBudget = conf.DefaultMemoryBudget
    94		}
    95		vm.memory = 0
    96		vm.ip = 0
    97	
    98		vm.fnArgsBuf = vm.fnArgsBuf[:0]
    99		vm.tries = vm.tries[:0]
   100		for vm.ip < len(program.Bytecode) {
   101			if debug && vm.debug {
   102				<-vm.step
   103			}
   104	
   105			op := program.Bytecode[vm.ip]
   106			arg := program.Arguments[vm.ip]
   107			vm.ip += 1
   108	
   109			if len(vm.tries) > 0 {
   110				func() {
   111					defer func() {
   112						if r := recover(); r != nil {
   113							vm.handlePanic(r)
   114						}
   115					}()
   116					vm.executeOp(program, env, op, arg)
   117				}()
   118			} else {
   119				vm.executeOp(program, env, op, arg)
   120			}
   121	
   122			if debug && vm.debug {
   123				vm.curr <- vm.ip
   124			}
   125		}
   126	
   127	
   128		if debug && vm.debug {
   129			close(vm.curr)
   130			close(vm.step)
=====TAIL=====
			for i := size - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
				array[i] = vm.pop()
			}
			vm.push(array)

		case OpMap:
			size := vm.pop().(int)
			vm.memGrow(uint(size))
			m := make(map[string]any)
			for i := size - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
				value := vm.pop()
				key := vm.pop()
				m[key.(string)] = value
			}
			vm.push(m)

		case OpLen:
			vm.push(runtime.Len(vm.current()))

		case OpCast:
			switch arg {
			case 0:
				vm.push(runtime.ToInt(vm.pop()))
			case 1:
				vm.push(runtime.ToInt64(vm.pop()))
			case 2:
				vm.push(runtime.ToFloat64(vm.pop()))
			case 3:
				vm.push(runtime.ToBool(vm.pop()))
			}

		case OpDeref:
			a := vm.pop()
			vm.push(deref.Interface(a))

		case OpIncrementIndex:
			vm.currScope.Index++

		case OpDecrementIndex:
			vm.currScope.Index--

		case OpIncrementCount:
			vm.currScope.Count++

		case OpGetIndex:
			vm.push(vm.currScope.Index)

		case OpGetCount:
			vm.push(vm.currScope.Count)

		case OpGetLen:
			vm.push(vm.currScope.Len)

		case OpGetAcc:
			vm.push(vm.currScope.Acc)

		case OpSetAcc:
			vm.currScope.Acc = vm.pop()

		case OpSetIndex:
			vm.currScope.Index = vm.pop().(int)

		case OpPointer:
			vm.push(vm.currScope.Item())

		case OpThrow:
			panic(vm.pop().(error))

		case OpCreate:
			switch arg {
			case 1:
				vm.push(make(groupBy))
			case 2:
				scope := vm.currScope
				var desc bool
				order, ok := vm.pop().(string)
				if !ok {
					panic("sortBy order argument must be a string")
				}
				switch order {
				case "asc":
					desc = false
				case "desc":
					desc = true
		case OpTry:
			vm.tries = append(vm.tries, tryFrame{
				tryIP:       vm.ip,
				catchIP:     arg,
				stackDepth:  len(vm.Stack),
				scopesDepth: len(vm.Scopes),
				variables:   append([]any(nil), vm.Variables...),
			})

		case OpEndTry:
			if len(vm.tries) == 0 {
				panic("OpEndTry without OpTry")
			}
			vm.tries = vm.tries[:len(vm.tries)-1]

		case OpEndCatch:
			if len(vm.tries) == 0 || !vm.tries[len(vm.tries)-1].inCatch {
				panic("OpEndCatch without active catch")
			}
			vm.tries = vm.tries[:len(vm.tries)-1]

		case OpRetry:
			vm.retry()

				default:
					panic("unknown order, use asc or desc")
				}
}

type tryFrame struct {
	tryIP       int
	catchIP     int
	stackDepth  int
	scopesDepth int
	variables   []any
	retries     int
	inCatch     bool
	name        string
	varIndex    int
	substr      string
}

const maxRetries = 3

func (vm *VM) handlePanic(r any) {
	err := runtime.AsError(r)
	message := err.Error()
	for i := len(vm.tries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
		frame := &vm.tries[i]
		if frame.inCatch {
			continue
		}
		if frame.substr != "" && !strings.Contains(message, frame.substr) {
			continue
		}
		// Discard inner try frames and keep this one for the catch handler.
		vm.tries = vm.tries[:i+1]
		frame = &vm.tries[i]
		frame.inCatch = true
		vm.restoreTryState(frame)
		if frame.name != "" {
			vm.Variables[frame.varIndex] = err
		}
		vm.ip = frame.catchIP
		return
	}
	panic(r)
}

func (vm *VM) restoreTryState(frame *tryFrame) {
	vm.Stack = vm.Stack[:frame.stackDepth]
	vm.Scopes = vm.Scopes[:frame.scopesDepth]
	if len(vm.Scopes) > 0 {
		vm.currScope = vm.Scopes[len(vm.Scopes)-1]
	} else {
		vm.currScope = nil
	}
	copy(vm.Variables, frame.variables)
}

func (vm *VM) retry() {
	for i := len(vm.tries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
		if vm.tries[i].inCatch {
			frame := &vm.tries[i]
			if frame.retries >= maxRetries {
				panic(runtime.ErrRetryExhausted)
			}
			frame.retries++
			frame.inCatch = false
			vm.restoreTryState(frame)
			vm.ip = frame.tryIP
			return
		}
	}
	panic("retry is not allowed outside a catch block")
}

	// Bytecode layout:
	//   OpTry <catchIP>
	//   <try body>
	//   OpEndTry
	//   OpJump <afterCatch>
	//   <catch body>
	//   OpEndCatch

	c.emit(OpEndTry)

	if node.Name != "" {
		index := c.addVariable(node.Name)
		c.beginScope(node.Name, index)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
		c.endScope()
	} else {
		c.compile(node.Catch)
	}

	c.patchJump(afterCatch)

		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpPop)

Wait, there's a problem with the tryIP. I declared `tryIP := len(c.bytecode)` and `_ = tryIP` but never use it in the compiler. Actually `tryIP` isn't
needed by the compiler at all — the VM computes it at runtime from vm.ip when OpTry executes. So I can remove the unused variable. Let me remove
`tryIP` and `_ = tryIP`.

Also, `opTry-1`: `opTry := c.emit(OpTry, placeholder)` returns the position (1-based index in bytecode after emit, i.e., `len(c.bytecode)` after
append). `c.arguments[opTry-1]` is the argument for OpTry. That's correct because `arguments` and `bytecode` are indexed the same, and `opTry` =
1-based index = index+1. So `opTry-1` is the index. Good.

But wait — the `catchIP` we store in the OpTry argument is `len(c.bytecode)` after emitting OpJump. But the OpJump's argument is a placeholder and
`patchJump` later adjusts `arguments[afterCatch-1]`. That's separate. Good.

Let me remove unused tryIP. Also, there's a subtle issue: the `OpEndTry` is emitted immediately after the try body, then `OpJump`. But on the success
path, OpEndTry pops the frame, then OpJump jumps to afterCatch. On the error path, the VM never executes OpEndTry (it jumps to catchIP). Good.

However, I realize there's a bug: on the success path, after `OpEndTry`, we jump to `afterCatch`. But `afterCatch` is after `OpEndCatch`. If there's a
finally, afterCatch is the finally body, which will push its result, then OpPop. The try result remains under the finally result. Good.

But wait: on the error path, the catch body leaves result, then OpEndCatch pops frame, then falls through to finally. Good.

Now, another issue: The VM's `handlePanic` sets `vm.ip = frame.catchIP`. The catch body is at catchIP. Good.

Now, let me remove the unused tryIP. Also, there's a potential issue: `c.arguments[opTry-1] = catchIP` — catchIP is an absolute index. But `opTry` is
emitted before `c.compile(node.Try)`, and `catchIP` is after `OpJump`. Good.

# append ErrType at end
s += '''
// ErrType classifies a caught error value.
func ErrType(arg any) any {
	if arg == nil {
		return "none"
	}
	var message string
	switch v := arg.(type) {
	case error:
		message = v.Error()
	default:
		message = fmt.Sprintf("%v", v)
	}

	// Errors produced by throw() are always custom.
	if _, ok := arg.(*runtime.RuntimeError); ok {
		return "custom"
	}

	msg := strings.ToLower(message)
	switch {
	case strings.Contains(msg, "out of range"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "out-of-range"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "bounds out of range"):
		return "index"
	case strings.Contains(msg, "conversion"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot convert"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid operation: int("),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid operation: int64("),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid operation: float("),
		strings.Contains(msg, "strconv."),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid syntax"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot parse"):
		return "conversion"
	case strings.Contains(msg, "nil pointer"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid memory address"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "is nil"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot call nil"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "nil map"):
		return "nil"
	case strings.Contains(msg, "retry"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "retry limit"):
		return "retry"
	case strings.Contains(msg, "interface conversion"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "type assertion"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot use"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "mismatched type"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "is not callable"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot call non-function"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "not defined on"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid argument for"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot fetch"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot get"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "cannot slice"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "no field"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "no method"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "invalid operation:"):
		return "type"
	default:
		return "custom"
	}
}
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("done")

package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"github.com/expr-lang/expr"
)

func main() {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
		"n": (*int)(nil),
	}
	run := func(name, input string, env any) {
		out, err := expr.Eval(input, env)
		fmt.Printf("%-28s => %#v, %v\n", name, out, err)
	}

	run("try success", `try(1+2, 99)`, env)
	run("try fallback", `try(1/0, 99)`, env)
	run("block named", `try { 1/0 } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("block named msg", `try { 1/0 } catch e { e }`, env)
	run("substr match", `try { a[10] } catch e is "out of range" { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("substr nomatch", `try { a[10] } catch e is "foo" { 111 }`, env)
	run("finally", `try { 1/0 } catch { 42 } finally { 7 }`, env)
	run("finally success", `try { 1+2 } catch { 42 } finally { 7 }`, env)
	run("finally throw", `try { 1/0 } catch { 42 } finally { throw("cleanup") }`, env)
	run("errtype index", `try { a[10] } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("errtype conv", `try { int("x") } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("errtype type", `try { 1 + "x" } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("errtype nil", `try { n.Foo } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("errtype custom", `try { throw("boom") } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("errtype none", `errtype(nil)`, env)
	run("retry outside", `retry()`, env)
	run("retry success", `let i = 0; try { if (i < 2) { i = 0; throw("x") } else { 42 } } catch e { retry() }`, env)
}
EOF

STEP 100 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Several issues:
1. `try(1/0, 99)` returned `+Inf` instead of fallback 99 — because in Go, `1/0` with integer literals? Actually `1/0` is integer division by zero. In
expr, `/` on ints returns float (`floatType`)? Let me check: the checker returns float for `/`, and compiler emits OpDivide → runtime.Divide which for
ints returns float64? Let me check runtime.Divide in helpers. It may return +Inf for integer division by zero rather than panic. So no error, hence no
fallback. That's expected behavior of expr — division by zero returns Inf. Need a different error to test fallback, e.g., `a[10]`.

2. `errtype` returned "none" even when catching errors — because the catch variable got... wait, for `try { a[10] } catch e { errtype(e) }` it
returned "none". That means `e` was nil! Why? Because in `handlePanic`, when `frame.name != ""`, we store error in `vm.Variables[frame.varIndex]`. But
then we call `vm.restoreTryState(frame)` BEFORE storing the error — restoreTryState copies `frame.variables` (snapshot) into vm.Variables, which
resets all variables including the catch variable to zero. Then we set `vm.Variables[frame.varIndex] = err`. That should work. But maybe
`frame.varIndex` isn't set! The compiler's TryNode with name calls `c.addVariable(node.Name)` but doesn't set `frame.varIndex` because the VM's
tryFrame doesn't know the var index — it's set at compile time and must be passed as part of the OpTry argument? Wait, I designed `tryFrame.varIndex`
but never populated it. The VM's OpTry creates the frame with catchIP=arg, but `varIndex` and `name` and `substr` are not passed! I need to encode
this info into the OpTry argument or use additional opcodes/arguments.

Right — the try frame needs to know:
- catchIP
- name (catch variable name) and varIndex
- substr filter

Currently OpTry's arg only holds catchIP. The compiler must pass name/varIndex/substr too. We could encode them as constants or as additional data in
the program. Simplest: extend OpTry to take multiple args? Opcodes only have one int arg. We can pack varIndex into the argument, and store
name/substr as constants... Hmm.

Alternative approach: Instead of storing varIndex/name/substr in the frame, we can have the compiler emit a separate opcode `OpCatchName <varIndex>`
or `OpCatchFilter <const>` at the catch start, and the VM sets frame fields when executing it. Let me think.

Actually, the VM's `handlePanic` decides whether to store the error. It needs to know the varIndex. We can pass varIndex as part of OpTry's argument
combined with catchIP? Not feasible since catchIP can be large.

Better: add a dedicated opcode `OpCatch` that is placed at the catch start and executed when entering catch (or when the VM jumps there). Wait, on

uses the pointer as map key. Fine. But if two TryInfos have same pointer? They're different pointers, so no dedup. Fine.

Now, `handlePanic` currently stores into variable; change to push err:
```go
func (vm *VM) handlePanic(r any) {
        err := runtime.AsError(r)
        message := err.Error()
        for i := len(vm.tries)-1; i >= 0; i-- {
                frame := &vm.tries[i]
                if frame.inCatch { continue }
                if frame.substr != "" && !strings.Contains(message, frame.substr) { continue }
                vm.tries = vm.tries[:i+1]
                frame = &vm.tries[i]
                frame.inCatch = true
                vm.restoreTryState(frame)
                vm.push(err)
                vm.ip = frame.catchIP
                return
        }
        panic(r)
}
```

And compiler catch start:
- if name: OpStore varIndex
- else: OpPop

This handles both named and unnamed. Good.

type TryInfo struct {
	CatchIP int    // Absolute bytecode position of the catch handler.
	Name    string // Catch variable name (empty if not bound).
	VarIndex int   // Variable index for the catch variable (-1 if not bound).
	Substr  string // Optional error message substring filter.
}

type tryFrame struct {
	tryIP       int
	catchIP     int
	stackDepth  int
	scopesDepth int
	variables   []any
	retries     int
	inCatch     bool
	name        string
	varIndex    int
	substr      string
}'''

MESSAGE
CALL s101_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_Mx6jBeChSKEenTs8wIdB5054
FUNCTION bash
ARGUMENTS
command:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='compiler/compiler.go'
s=open(p).read()
old='''func (c *compiler) TryNode(node *ast.TryNode) {
	// Bytecode layout:
	//   OpTry <catchIP>
	//   <try body>
	//   OpEndTry
	//   OpJump <afterCatch>
	//   <catch body>
	//   OpEndCatch
	//   afterCatch: [<finally body> OpPop]
	opTry := c.emit(OpTry, placeholder)

	c.compile(node.Try)
	c.emit(OpEndTry)

	afterCatch := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)
	catchIP := len(c.bytecode)

	c.arguments[opTry-1] = catchIP // patch OpTry argument with absolute catch position

	if node.Name != "" {
		index := c.addVariable(node.Name)
		c.beginScope(node.Name, index)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
		c.endScope()
	} else {
		c.compile(node.Catch)
	}
	c.emit(OpEndCatch)
	c.patchJump(afterCatch)

	if node.Finally != nil {
		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpPop)
	}

}'''
new='''func (c *compiler) TryNode(node *ast.TryNode) {
	// Bytecode layout:
	//   OpTry <TryInfo constant>
	//   <try body>
	//   OpEndTry
	//   OpJump <afterCatch>
	//   catchIP: OpStore <varIndex> | OpPop
	//   <catch body>
	//   OpEndCatch
	//   afterCatch: [<finally body> OpPop]
	varIndex := -1
	if node.Name != "" {
		varIndex = c.addVariable(node.Name)
	}
	opTry := c.emit(OpTry, placeholder)

	c.compile(node.Try)
	c.emit(OpEndTry)

	afterCatch := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)
	catchIP := len(c.bytecode)

	c.arguments[opTry-1] = c.addConstant(&TryInfo{
		CatchIP:  catchIP,
		Name:     node.Name,
		VarIndex: varIndex,
		Substr:   node.Substr,
	})

	if node.Name != "" {
		c.emit(OpStore, varIndex)
		c.beginScope(node.Name, varIndex)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
		c.endScope()
	} else {
		c.emit(OpPop)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
	}
	c.emit(OpEndCatch)
	c.patchJump(afterCatch)

	if node.Finally != nil {
		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpPop)
	}

}'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("done")

package main

import (
	"fmt"

func main() {
	env := map[string]any{

		"n": (*int)(nil),
	}
	run := func(name, input string, env any) {
		out, err := expr.Eval(input, env)
		fmt.Printf("%-28s => %#v, %v\n", name, out, err)
	}

	run("try success", `try(1+2, 99)`, env)

	run("block named type", `try { a[10] } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("block named msg", `try { a[10] } catch e { e }`, env)

	run("finally success", `try { 1+2 } catch { 42 } finally { 7 }`, env)
	run("finally throw", `try { a[10] } catch { 42 } finally { throw("cleanup") }`, env)
	run("errtype index", `try { a[10] } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	run("errtype conv", `try { int("x") } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)

	run("retry outside", `retry()`, env)
	run("throw count", `let i = 0; try { i = i + 1; throw("x") } catch e { if (i < 3) { retry() } else { i } }`, env)
	run("retry exhaustion", `let i = 0; try { i = i + 1; throw("x") } catch e { retry() }`, env)
}

package deref

import (
	"fmt"
	"reflect"
)

func Interface(p any) any {
	if p == nil {
		return nil
	}

	v := reflect.ValueOf(p)

	for v.Kind() == reflect.Ptr || v.Kind() == reflect.Interface {
		if v.IsNil() {
			return nil
		}
		v = v.Elem()
	}

	if v.IsValid() {
		return v.Interface()
	}

	panic(fmt.Sprintf("cannot dereference %v", p))
}

func Type(t reflect.Type) reflect.Type {
	if t == nil {
		return nil
	}
	for t.Kind() == reflect.Ptr {
		t = t.Elem()
	}
	return t
}

func Value(v reflect.Value) reflect.Value {
	for v.Kind() == reflect.Ptr || v.Kind() == reflect.Interface {
		if v.IsNil() {
			return v
		}
		v = v.Elem()
	}
	return v
}

func TypeKind(t reflect.Type, k reflect.Kind) (_ reflect.Type, _ reflect.Kind, changed bool) {
	for k == reflect.Pointer {
		changed = true
		t = t.Elem()
		k = t.Kind()
	}
	return t, k, changed
}

CALL s108_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_lYF2VDrIDYyF6qg7KUih6672

cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='vm/runtime/runtime.go'
s=open(p).read()

# Fetch: add nil check after reflect.ValueOf
old='''func Fetch(from, i any) any {
	v := reflect.ValueOf(from)
	if v.Kind() == reflect.Invalid {
		panic(fmt.Sprintf("cannot fetch %v from %T", i, from))
	}
'''
new='''func Fetch(from, i any) any {
	v := reflect.ValueOf(from)
	if v.Kind() == reflect.Invalid {
		panic(fmt.Sprintf("cannot fetch %v from %T", i, from))
	}
	if (v.Kind() == reflect.Ptr || v.Kind() == reflect.Interface) && v.IsNil() {
		panic(fmt.Sprintf("cannot fetch %v from nil %T", i, from))
	}
'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

# FetchField: add nil check
old='''func FetchField(from any, field *Field) any {
	v := reflect.ValueOf(from)
	if v.Kind() != reflect.Invalid {
		v = reflect.Indirect(v)
'''
new='''func FetchField(from any, field *Field) any {
	v := reflect.ValueOf(from)
	if v.Kind() != reflect.Invalid {
		if (v.Kind() == reflect.Ptr || v.Kind() == reflect.Interface) && v.IsNil() {
			panic(fmt.Sprintf("cannot get %v from nil %T", field.Path[0], from))
		}
		v = reflect.Indirect(v)
'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

# FetchMethod: add nil check
old='''func FetchMethod(from any, method *Method) any {
	v := reflect.ValueOf(from)
	kind := v.Kind()
	if kind != reflect.Invalid {
'''
new='''func FetchMethod(from any, method *Method) any {
	v := reflect.ValueOf(from)
	kind := v.Kind()
	if kind != reflect.Invalid {
		if (kind == reflect.Ptr || kind == reflect.Interface) && v.IsNil() {
			panic(fmt.Sprintf("cannot fetch %v from nil %T", method.Name, from))
		}
'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

open(p,'w').write(s)
print("runtime done")

catch calls retry; initial attempt (retries=0) throws, retry→retries=1; second attempt throws, retry→2; third attempt throws, retry→3; fourth attempt
throws, retry→ `frame.retries >= 3` (3>=3) → panic exhaustion. So 4 attempts total, 3 retries. Good.

Let me redesign handlePanic:
```
func (vm *VM) handlePanic(r any) {
        err := runtime.AsError(r)
        message := err.Error()
        for i := len(vm.tries)-1; i >= 0; i-- {
                frame := &vm.tries[i]
                if frame.inCatch {
                        // The catch handler itself threw. If it has a finally, run it
                        // before propagating.
                        if frame.finallyIP >= 0 {
                                // discard inner frames, run this frame's finally
                                vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]
                                vm.restoreTryState(frame)
                                vm.pendingErr = err
                                vm.ip = frame.finallyIP
                                return
                        }
                        continue
                }
                if frame.substr != "" && !strings.Contains(message, frame.substr) {
                        continue
                }
                // normal catch
                vm.tries = vm.tries[:i+1]
                frame = &vm.tries[i]
                frame.inCatch = true
                vm.restoreTryState(frame)
                vm.push(err)
                vm.ip = frame.catchIP
                return
        }
        panic(r)
}
```

Wait, for the inCatch-with-finally case: we truncate `vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]` (discard this frame and all inner). Then restore state. Set pendingErr
and jump to finallyIP. The frame is gone, so when finally finishes, OpEndFinally re-panics pendingErr. But note: there may be an outer frame that also
has inCatch... Actually if outer catch is also inCatch, its finally should also run. But we only run the innermost inCatch frame's finally, then
re-panic; the re-panic would then trigger the next outer inCatch frame's finally via handlePanic. Wait, but after OpEndFinally re-panics, that panic
occurs while we're inside the outer catch (but the inner frame was removed). The outer frame may be inCatch=true with finally. handlePanic would then
handle it. But we've removed the inner frame; the outer frame is still in vm.tries. So the re-panic from OpEndFinally would be caught by the
per-opcode recover wrapper (since len(vm.tries)>0), and handlePanic would find the outer inCatch frame and run its finally. This cascades correctly!
Good.

		if frame.inCatch {
			continue
		}

new='''func (vm *VM) handlePanic(r any) {
	err := runtime.AsError(r)
	message := err.Error()
	vm.pendingErr = nil
	for i := len(vm.tries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
		frame := &vm.tries[i]
		if frame.inCatch {
			// The catch handler itself threw. If it has a finally block,
			// run it before propagating the new error.
			if frame.finallyIP >= 0 {
				vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]
				vm.restoreTryState(frame)
				vm.pendingErr = err
				vm.ip = frame.finallyIP
				return
			}
			continue
		}
		if frame.substr != "" && !strings.Contains(message, frame.substr) {
			// The catch filter did not match. Run the finally block before
			// propagating the error to an outer handler.
			if frame.finallyIP >= 0 {
				vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]
				vm.restoreTryState(frame)
				vm.pendingErr = err
				vm.ip = frame.finallyIP

		frame.inCatch = true
		vm.restoreTryState(frame)
		vm.push(err)
		vm.ip = frame.catchIP
		return
	}
	panic(r)
}'''

REASONING_CONTENT
Now update compiler TryNode for the new layout with finally and OpEndFinally. Let me rewrite the TryNode method.
MESSAGE
CALL s117_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_LarWEzgjPaQxaN3A6XSy8889
FUNCTION bash
ARGUMENTS
command:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='compiler/compiler.go'
s=open(p).read()
old='''func (c *compiler) TryNode(node *ast.TryNode) {
	// Bytecode layout:
	//   OpTry <TryInfo constant>
	//   <try body>
	//   OpEndTry
	//   OpJump <afterCatch>
	//   catchIP: OpStore <varIndex> | OpPop
	//   <catch body>
	//   OpEndCatch
	//   afterCatch: [<finally body> OpPop]
	varIndex := -1
	if node.Name != "" {
		varIndex = c.addVariable(node.Name)
	}
	opTry := c.emit(OpTry, placeholder)

	c.compile(node.Try)
	c.emit(OpEndTry)

	afterCatch := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)
	catchIP := len(c.bytecode)

	c.arguments[opTry-1] = c.addConstant(&TryInfo{
		CatchIP:  catchIP,
		Name:     node.Name,
		VarIndex: varIndex,
		Substr:   node.Substr,
	})

	if node.Name != "" {
		c.emit(OpStore, varIndex)
		c.beginScope(node.Name, varIndex)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
		c.endScope()
	} else {
		c.emit(OpPop)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
	}
	c.emit(OpEndCatch)
	c.patchJump(afterCatch)

	if node.Finally != nil {
		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpPop)
	}

}'''
new='''func (c *compiler) TryNode(node *ast.TryNode) {
	// Bytecode layout:
	//   OpTry <TryInfo constant>
	//   <try body>
	//   OpEndTry
	//   OpJump <finallyOrEnd>
	//   catchIP: OpStore <varIndex> | OpPop
	//   <catch body>
	//   OpEndCatch
	//   OpJump <finallyOrEnd>
	//   finallyIP: <finally body> OpEndFinally
	varIndex := -1
	if node.Name != "" {
		varIndex = c.addVariable(node.Name)
	}
	opTry := c.emit(OpTry, placeholder)

	c.compile(node.Try)
	c.emit(OpEndTry)

	successJump := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)
	catchIP := len(c.bytecode)

	if node.Name != "" {
		c.emit(OpStore, varIndex)
		c.beginScope(node.Name, varIndex)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
		c.endScope()
	} else {
		c.emit(OpPop)
		c.compile(node.Catch)
	}
	c.emit(OpEndCatch)
	catchJump := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)

	finallyIP := -1
	if node.Finally != nil {
		finallyIP = len(c.bytecode)
		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpEndFinally)
	}
	c.patchJump(successJump)
	c.patchJump(catchJump)

	c.arguments[opTry-1] = c.addConstant(&TryInfo{
		CatchIP:   catchIP,
		FinallyIP: finallyIP,
		Name:      node.Name,
		VarIndex:  varIndex,
		Substr:    node.Substr,
	})
}'''

Actually the jump offset semantics: `OpJump` does `vm.ip += arg` where `vm.ip` has already been incremented past OpJump. So target = (jump_index+1) +
arg. In compiler, `patchJump(placeholder)` computes `offset := len(c.bytecode) - placeholder` (where placeholder is the 1-based index of the jump
instruction) and sets `arguments[placeholder-1] = offset`. For a jump at position p (1-based), after emitting OpJump, len(c.bytecode)=p, and vm.ip
after executing = p (since 0-based index p means it's the (p+1)-th... let me not overthink; `patchJump` is the standard.

To make successJump target finallyIP (0-based index of finally body), the offset should be `finallyIP - p` where p is the 0-based index of the jump
instruction? Let me derive from `patchJump`: `placeholder` is 1-based index (returned by emit = len(c.bytecode) after emit, which is 1-based count).
`offset = len(c.bytecode) - placeholder`. At patch time, len(c.bytecode) = current count (1-based). Target 0-based = current count - 1 + offset? Let's
just use the existing helper differently: we can emit the finally, get `end`, then patch to end (current behavior works for no-finally end), but for
finally we need to target finallyIP.

Simplest: after computing finallyIP (before compiling finally), manually set:
```go
if node.Finally != nil {
        finallyIP = len(c.bytecode)
        // patch successJump and catchJump to finallyIP
        c.arguments[successJump-1] = finallyIP - successJump
        c.arguments[catchJump-1] = finallyIP - catchJump
        c.compile(node.Finally)
        c.emit(OpEndFinally)
}
```
Let me verify offset: Let p = successJump (1-based index of OpJump). After VM increments ip past OpJump, vm.ip = p (0-based index of next instruction
= p, since instructions are 0-based and count p means indices 0..p-1; after reading instruction at index p-1, ip becomes p). So target 0-based = p +
arg. We want target = finallyIP (0-based). So arg = finallyIP - p. Yes.

For no finally, we want both jumps to target the end: arg = len(c.bytecode) - p (current end). Using `patchJump(successJump)` after all compilation
gives exactly that. Good.

CALL s124_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_MSIopQhbo4Iafg9lAtQ03903

				// Discard the finally body result.
				if len(vm.Stack) > 0 {
					vm.pop()
				}
				if vm.pendingErr != nil {

old='''	finallyIP := -1

		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpEndFinally)

		// Both success and normal catch completion jump to the finally block.
		c.arguments[successJump-1] = finallyIP - successJump
		c.arguments[catchJump-1] = finallyIP - catchJump
		c.compile(node.Finally)
		c.emit(OpEndFinally)
	} else {
		c.patchJump(successJump)
		c.patchJump(catchJump)
	}'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
open(p,'w').write(s)

package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"github.com/expr-lang/expr"
)

func main() {
	count := 0
	env := map[string]any{
		"fail": func() any {
			count++
			if count < 3 { panic("boom") }
			return count
		},
	}
	out, err := expr.Eval(`try { fail() } catch e { retry() } finally { 99 }`, env)
	fmt.Printf("retry+finally => out=%#v err=%v count=%d\n", out, err, count)

	count = 0
	out, err = expr.Eval(`try { fail() } catch e { retry() }`, env)
	fmt.Printf("retry no finally => out=%#v err=%v count=%d\n", out, err, count)
}

The `try in filter` parse error: `try(int("1"), 0) == 1` — the parse of `try(...)` function form consumed the `)` and then `== 1`, but the error says
unexpected token `==` at position after `try(int("1"), 0)`? Hmm. It says unexpected token Operator("==") at (1:28). Let me count: `filter(a,
try(int("1"), 0) == 1)`. Position 28 is around the `==`. Why would `==` be unexpected?

The issue: `filter` is a predicate builtin; the second argument is parsed as a predicate via `parsePredicate()`. Inside predicate, `try(...)` is
parsed. `try(int("1"), 0)` should parse fine. Then `== 1` should be a binary comparison. But the parser error suggests that after parsing `try(...)`,
it didn't continue with `==`.

Wait — maybe the problem is that `try` is parsed in `parsePrimary` and returns a TryNode, but then `parseExpression`'s operator loop doesn't continue
because... Let me trace: `parsePredicate` calls `parseExpression(0)`. In `parseExpression(0)`, at precedence 0, it checks `p.current.Is(Operator,
"try")` → calls `p.parseTry()`. parseTry consumes `try(...)`. It returns. Then back in parseExpression, after parsePrimary (wait, we called parseTry
directly from parseExpression and returned it!). That's the bug: in `parseExpression`, I added:
```go
if precedence == 0 && p.current.Is(Operator, "try") {
        return p.parseTry()
}
```
This RETURNS immediately, so the operator loop (which handles `== 1`) is skipped! For `try { ... }` as a standalone expression, that's fine (nothing
follows). But `try(...) == 1` would also fail. And in parsePrimary I added try handling, which returns parseTry() and then parsePostfixExpression is
NOT called (parsePrimary returns parseTry directly, skipping postfix). Actually for `try(...) == 1`, parsePrimary is not reached because
parseExpression's precedence-0 branch returns parseTry immediately.

I should make parseTry go through the normal expression flow so operators after it are handled. In parseExpression, instead of `return p.parseTry()`,
I should set `nodeLeft = p.parseTry()` and fall through to the operator loop. Similarly in parsePrimary, parseTry returns a node and then
parsePostfixExpression should be applied? Actually the operator loop in parseExpression handles binary operators, while parsePostfixExpression handles
postfix (`.foo`, `[i]`, method calls). For `try { } .foo`? Probably not needed but `try(...)[0]` might be. Let me handle:

In parseExpression:
```go
if precedence == 0 && p.current.Is(Operator, "try") {
        nodeLeft = p.parseTry()
} else {
        nodeLeft = p.parsePrimary()
}
```
But careful: if parseTry returns, then we skip parsePrimary (which also handles try). But then the code proceeds to the operator loop. Good.

In parsePrimary, for the precedence>0 case (`1 + try(...)`), parsePrimary is called; it sees try and returns parseTry(). Then in parseExpression,
after parsePrimary, the operator loop continues (because parsePrimary's return goes back to parseExpression which then has the operator loop). Wait —
in parseExpression, after `nodeLeft := p.parsePrimary()`, it enters the operator loop. So if parsePrimary returns a TryNode, the loop handles `==`. So
parsePrimary's try handling is fine.

The bug is specifically the early `return p.parseTry()` in parseExpression. Let me fix it to assign instead of return.

REASONING_CONTENT
All good. Now let's think about a few more subtle issues:

1. **`retry()` compiled via BuiltinNode**: In compiler, I added special-case `if node.Name == "retry"` at the top of the generic builtin section. But
there's also the `predicates` map — retry isn't in predicates, so it goes through parseCall → builtin.Index → BuiltinNode. Good. The checker for
retry: `builtin.Index["retry"]` exists, so `builtinNode` reaches `checkFunction` with f.Validate. It returns anyType. Good.

2. **`throw` and `retry` disabled via DisableBuiltin**: If a user disables `throw`, then parser won't create BuiltinNode (because parseCall checks
`!p.config.Disabled[token.Value]`). But `retry` special-case in compiler would still fire if the parser creates BuiltinNode... if disabled, parser
creates CallNode (custom function) instead, so no issue. Good.

3. **`errtype` Fast builtin and deref**: The generic compiler path for Fast builtins: it compiles args, emits OpDeref if arg type is Ptr/unknown. For
`errtype(e)` where e is any (error interface), `e.Type()` is interface, not Ptr, and Nature unknown? `arg.Nature().IsUnknown` — if e is `anyType`,
Nature is interface with type any, not unknown. So no OpDeref. At runtime, OpCallBuiltin1 pops e and calls ErrType(e). Good.

4. **Error variable type**: I set catch var nature to `FromType(anyType)`. If user does `errtype(e)` it works. If user does `e.Error()` — since e is
any, checker would see unknown method on any → maybe compile error. But `e.Error()` isn't needed per spec. Fine.

5. **`errtype` with `*file.Error`**: When catch receives `runtime.AsError(r)`, for a panic that is an `error` (like from OpCall), it returns the
original error (e.g., `*RuntimeError` or `errors.errorString`), not `*file.Error`. So errtype works on the raw error. Good.

6. **What about panics that are not error/string**, e.g., `panic(42)` from a custom Go function? `runtime.AsError` converts to `errors.New("42")`.
`catch e { errtype(e) }` → "custom". Good.

7. **`try` with `finally` and no catch?** Not allowed by parser (catch required). Fine.

8. **Memory budget**: `vm.memory` isn't restored on retry; acceptable.

9. **`vm.fnArgsBuf` state after panic/retry**: If a function call panics after `getArgsForFunc` advanced the buffer, on retry the buffer pointer
continues; getArgsForFunc handles insufficient length by allocating. Good.

10. **`vm.Scopes` restore with `scopePoolIdx`**: Not restored, but scope pool slots beyond idx are just unused. On retry, new scopes allocated from
later pool slots. Fine.

11. **`OpEndTry` on success path after a try body that itself jumped** — fine.

12. **`OpEndCatch` when catch calls retry and retry succeeds**: On retry success, we go through OpEndTry (pops frame) and OpJump; OpEndCatch is never
executed. Frame was the one marked inCatch→false by retry; OpEndTry pops it. Good.

13. **`OpEndCatch` if catch body ends normally**: pops frame. Good.

14. **Double OpEndCatch?** No.

15. **Nested finally propagation cascade**: Let me test the scenario where catch throws inside a try with finally, inside another try with finally, to
ensure cascading pendingErr works. Let me test.

16. **`try` inside `finally`** — should work recursively.

Let me test cascade.

not int". Classified as "type" via "interface conversion". Good.

Now, a concern: in the per-opcode recover wrapper, if a panic occurs inside a try and handlePanic re-panics (no matching frame), the re-panic goes to
the top-level defer. But what if there are outer frames that are inCatch and have no finally? They're skipped. If no frame catches, re-panic. Good.

Another concern: **Panics during `handlePanic` itself** (e.g., `vm.push(err)` on stack?) — unlikely.

Another concern: **`vm.tries` leak if program ends while frame active** — shouldn't happen because every try has OpEndTry/OpEndCatch on normal paths.
If a panic propagates to top-level, Run returns error; tries reset next run. Good.

Now, let me consider **compiler optimize()** interaction with OpTry's constant argument. `optimize()` only processes jump opcodes, not OpTry. The
`OpTry` arg is a constant index; fine.

Now, **patcher/optimizer folding try nodes**: Ast.Walk handles TryNode, so visitors traverse. Some optimizer may fold constants inside try body (e.g.,
`try(1+2, 0)` → fold to `try(3,0)`). Fine.

cd /app && cat > error_handling_test.go <<'EOF'
package expr_test

import (
	"fmt"
	"testing"

	"github.com/expr-lang/expr"
	"github.com/expr-lang/expr/internal/testify/require"
)

type errorTestS struct {
	Foo int
}

func evalErr(t *testing.T, input string, env any) (any, error) {
	t.Helper()
	program, err := expr.Compile(input, expr.Env(env), expr.AllowUndefinedVariables())
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return expr.Run(program, env)
}

func TestTryFunctionForm(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try(1 + 2, 99)`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 3, out)

	out, err = evalErr(t, `try(a[10], 99)`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 99, out)

	// The fallback is lazy: it must not be evaluated on success.
	out, err = evalErr(t, `try(1, throw("must not run"))`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 1, out)

	// An error thrown by the fallback propagates.
	_, err = evalErr(t, `try(a[10], throw("fallback"))`, env)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "fallback")
}

func TestTryArity(t *testing.T) {
	_, err := expr.Compile(`try(1)`)
	require.Error(t, err)

	_, err = expr.Compile(`try(1, 2, 3)`)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "exactly two arguments")
}

func TestTryBlockForm(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { 1 + 2 } catch { 99 }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 3, out)

	out, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch { 99 }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 99, out)

	out, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch err { errtype(err) }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "index", out)
}

func TestTrySubstringFilter(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch e is "out of range" { errtype(e) }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "index", out)

	// Non-matching filter propagates the error.
	_, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch e is "not this" { 42 }`, env)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "out of range")
}

func TestThrow(t *testing.T) {
	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { throw("boom") } catch e { errtype(e) }`, nil)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "custom", out)

	_, err = evalErr(t, `throw("boom")`, nil)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "boom")

	// throw requires exactly one argument.
	_, err = expr.Compile(`throw()`)
	require.Error(t, err)
	_, err = expr.Compile(`throw(1, 2)`)
	require.Error(t, err)
}

func TestErrType(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
		"s": (*errorTestS)(nil),
	}

	tests := []struct {
		input string
		want  string
	}{
		{`try { a[10] } catch e { errtype(e) }`, "index"},
		{`try { int("x") } catch e { errtype(e) }`, "conversion"},
		{`try { 1 + "x" } catch e { errtype(e) }`, "type"},
		{`try { s.Foo } catch e { errtype(e) }`, "nil"},
		{`try { throw("boom") } catch e { errtype(e) }`, "custom"},
		{`errtype(nil)`, "none"},
	}

	for _, tt := range tests {
		out, err := evalErr(t, tt.input, env)
		require.NoError(t, err)
		require.Equal(t, tt.want, out, tt.input)
	}
}

func TestFinally(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
		"log": func(s string) any {
			fmt.Println("cleanup:", s)
			return s
		},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { 1 + 2 } catch { 0 } finally { log("fin") }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 3, out)

	out, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch { 7 } finally { log("fin") }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 7, out)

	// If the finally body throws, that error overrides the prior result.
	_, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch { 7 } finally { throw("cleanup failed") }`, env)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "cleanup failed")
}

func TestRetry(t *testing.T) {
	count := 0
	env := map[string]any{
		"fail": func() any {
			count++
			if count < 3 {
				panic("not yet")
			}
			return count
		},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { fail() } catch e { retry() }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 3, out)
	require.Equal(t, 3, count)
}

func TestRetryExhaustion(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"fail": func() any { panic("always") },
	}

	_, err := evalErr(t, `try { fail() } catch e { retry() }`, env)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "retry limit exceeded")

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { try { fail() } catch e { retry() } } catch e2 { errtype(e2) }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "retry", out)
}

func TestRetryOutsideCatch(t *testing.T) {
	_, err := evalErr(t, `retry()`, nil)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "outside a catch")
}

func TestNestedTry(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { try { a[10] } catch e is "no match" { 0 } } catch e { errtype(e) }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "index", out)
}

func TestTryInExpression(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `1 + try(a[10], 41)`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, 42, out)

	out, err = evalErr(t, `try(a[10], 1) == 1`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, true, out)

	out, err = evalErr(t, `filter(a, try(int("1"), 0) == 1)`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, []any{1, 2, 3}, out)
}

func TestTryScopeRestore(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
	}

	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { count(a, int("x") == 1) } catch e { "caught" }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "caught", out)
}

REASONING_CONTENT
All tests pass. Now let me think about additional robustness and spec compliance:

1. **`try(expression, fallback)` requires exactly two arguments** — done.
2. **`try { expr } catch { handler }`** — done.
3. **`catch <name> { ... }`** — done.
4. **`catch <name> is "substring" { ... }`** — done.
5. **`finally { cleanup }` optional, always executes, if throws overrides** — done (including catch-throws and filter-mismatch).
6. **`throw(value)` requires exactly one arg; message is string conversion** — done. Message uses `fmt.Sprintf("%v", args[0])`. For nil, `%v` gives
`<nil>`. Fine.
7. **`retry` usable inside catch, max 3 retries, distinct exhaustion error, outside catch runtime error** — done.
8. **`errtype(err)` requires exactly one arg; classifications** — done.

Let me now consider a few more edge cases:

### `retry` arity
The task says "`retry` - usable inside catch blocks". It doesn't explicitly say `retry()` with zero args, but the syntax is likely `retry()`. Our
implementation requires exactly 0 args (Validate). The compiler special-case panics if args != 0 (but checker prevents). Good.

### `try` function form with whitespace `try (a, b)`
`try` is an operator; after `p.next()` current is `(` regardless of whitespace. Good.

### `try` block with nested braces
`parseSequenceExpression` handles nested braces inside. Good.

### `finally` with no catch? Not allowed. Good.

### `catch` variable name conflict with existing variable
If a `let` variable has the same name, the catch scope shadows it via varScopes and compiler beginScope. At compile time, `addVariable(node.Name)`
adds a NEW variable index (not reusing existing), and beginScope shadows. So `let err = 1; try {} catch err { err }` — the catch err is a different
variable. At runtime, handlePanic writes to the new var index; restoreTryState snapshots variables at OpTry (after let executed), so the let value is
in the snapshot; catch err variable gets the error. Good.

But wait: `let err = 1; try {} catch err { ... }` — the catch var index is allocated at compile time as a new variable slot. The `let err` also has
its own slot. When `OpTry` executes, the stack depth snapshot etc. includes the let value already stored in vm.Variables[letIndex]. The catch var slot
is zero at that point. On error, restoreTryState copies snapshot (let value preserved, catch var zero), then OpStore writes error to catch var. Good.

### `try` inside `let` value
`let x = try(a[10], 1); x` — should work. Let me test quickly.

### `finally` with a `let` declaration
`try {} catch {} finally { let y = 1; y }` — the finally result is discarded; the let declaration inside finally allocates a variable and stores it;
OpEndFinally pops the result. Fine.

### Optimization interplay: `optimizer` might replace `try(1, 2)` with constant? It uses Walk and patchWithType. `patchWithType` has a `default:
panic` for unknown node types! If an optimizer tries to patch a TryNode with patchWithType, it would panic. Let me check: patchWithType is called only
when folding specific node types into constants/binary. It won't be called on TryNode itself. But `patchCopyType` could be called on any node? Let me
search where patchCopyType is called with an arbitrary node. In fold.go, patchCopyType is called for certain patterns. It won't be called on TryNode
because the switch cases only match specific node types (Unary, Binary, etc.) and TryNode isn't one. But `Walk` traverses into TryNode children; the
children could be folded. That's fine.

### `constant expr` optimizer (ConstExpr)
It might evaluate try? No.

### Profiling
Compiler's `compile` wraps each node with OpProfileStart/End if config.Profile. For TryNode, it would wrap the whole try in profile spans. That's
fine.

Let me test a few more edge cases: let + try, retry inside predicate catch, try in let.

Great.

Now, let's reconsider the `errtype` classification of errors from custom user functions. A user function returning an `error` will cause OpCall to
panic with that error. If message contains "index out of range", errtype returns "index". That's reasonable.

Now, one more important thing: **The `try` keyword in lexer as Operator means `try` can't be used as an identifier**, which is intended. But what
about `DisableIfOperator` — `try` still operator. Good.

Now, let me also check **`retry` as a variable name** — since retry is a builtin, `retry` alone (without parens) as identifier would be treated as
unknown name unless env has it. But if env has `retry` field, the parser sees Identifier "retry" followed by not `(` → IdentifierNode; the checker
would resolve to env. That's fine. If `retry()` is called, it's the builtin (unless overridden by custom function via expr.Function). Acceptable.

Now, let me think about **`throw` string conversion for non-string values**: `throw(42)` message "42"; `throw(nil)` message "<nil>". Good.

Now, let me examine whether the **top-level error for uncaught errors inside a `try` with no matching catch** is correctly wrapped with source
location. In repro14, `catch e is "bar"` no match → error shows location (1:7) pointing at `throw`. That's because handlePanic re-panics, and
top-level recover uses `vm.ip-1` at the time of the re-panic. But wait — after handlePanic re-panics, `vm.ip` is still pointing to the instruction
that threw (the OpCall1), because handlePanic doesn't change ip when re-panicking. So location is correct. Good.

Now, let me consider **memory/performance**: The per-opcode closure allocates each opcode while inside try. For typical expressions it's fine. But
could be a performance concern. Given correctness over performance, acceptable. But maybe we can optimize by only wrapping when there's a try, which
we do.

Now, let me also think about **`OpEndFinally` and `vm.pendingErr` clearing when finally body runs normally**. After OpEndFinally, pendingErr is nil
(or was re-raised). Good.

Now, a subtle issue: **When handlePanic sets pendingErr and jumps to finally, and the finally body itself contains a try/catch that catches the... no,
the finally body's inner try is a separate frame; the pendingErr is a VM field, not an exception on the stack. The inner try won't see it. After inner
try completes normally (its own OpEndFinally pops its result), the outer finally body continues, then outer OpEndFinally sees pendingErr and
re-panics. Good. But what if the finally body's inner try catches something and calls retry? That retry targets the inner try, fine.

Now, another subtle issue: **`vm.pendingErr` when a finally body throws and the error is caught by an outer try**. handlePanic clears pendingErr at
start, so the stale pendingErr is cleared. Then the outer try catches the finally's error. Good.

Let me test: `try { try { a[10] } catch { 1 } finally { throw("fin") } } catch e { errtype(e) }`. The inner finally throws "fin" → caught by outer →
errtype custom. Let me verify.

    39	type VM struct {
    40		Stack        []any
    41		Scopes       []*Scope
    42		Variables    []any
    43		MemoryBudget uint
    44		ip           int
    45		memory       uint
    46		debug        bool

    50		scopePoolIdx int     // Current index into scopePool for allocation
    51		currScope    *Scope  // Cached pointer to the current scope (optimization)
    52		fnArgsBuf    []any   // Reusable buffer for function call arguments
    53		tries        []tryFrame
    54		pendingErr   error // Error to re-raise after a finally block completes.

    56	
    57	func (vm *VM) Run(program *Program, env any) (_ any, err error) {
    58		defer func() {
    59			if r := recover(); r != nil {
    60				var location file.Location
    61				if vm.ip-1 < len(program.locations) {
    62					location = program.locations[vm.ip-1]
    63				}
    64				f := &file.Error{
    65					Location: location,
    66					Message:  fmt.Sprintf("%v", r),
    67				}
    68				if err, ok := r.(error); ok {
    69					f.Wrap(err)
    70				}
    71				err = f.Bind(program.source)
    72			}
    73		}()
    74	
    75		if vm.Stack == nil {
    76			vm.Stack = make([]any, 0, 2)
    77		} else {
    78			clearSlice(vm.Stack)
    79			vm.Stack = vm.Stack[0:0]
    80		}
    81		if vm.Scopes != nil {
    82			clearSlice(vm.Scopes)
    83			vm.Scopes = vm.Scopes[0:0]
    84		}
    85		vm.scopePoolIdx = 0 // Reset pool index for reuse
    86		vm.currScope = nil
    87		if len(vm.Variables) < program.variables {
    88			vm.Variables = make([]any, program.variables)
    89		}
    90		if vm.MemoryBudget == 0 {
    91			vm.MemoryBudget = conf.DefaultMemoryBudget
    92		}
    93		vm.memory = 0
    94		vm.ip = 0
    95	
    96		vm.fnArgsBuf = vm.fnArgsBuf[:0]
    97		vm.tries = vm.tries[:0]
    98		vm.pendingErr = nil
    99		for vm.ip < len(program.Bytecode) {
   100			if debug && vm.debug {
   101				<-vm.step
   102			}
   103	
   104			op := program.Bytecode[vm.ip]
   105			arg := program.Arguments[vm.ip]
   106			vm.ip += 1
   107	
   108			if len(vm.tries) > 0 {
   109				func() {
   110					defer func() {
   111						if r := recover(); r != nil {
   112							vm.handlePanic(r)
   113						}
   114					}()
   115					vm.executeOp(program, env, op, arg)
   116				}()
   117			} else {
   118				vm.executeOp(program, env, op, arg)
   119			}
   120	
   121			if debug && vm.debug {
   122				vm.curr <- vm.ip
   123			}
   124		}
   125	
   126		if debug && vm.debug {
   127			close(vm.curr)
   128			close(vm.step)
   129		}
   130	
   131		if len(vm.Stack) > 0 {
   132			return vm.pop(), nil
   133		}
   134	
   135		return nil, nil
   136	}
   137	
   138	func (vm *VM) push(value any) {
   139		vm.Stack = append(vm.Stack, value)
   140	}
   141	
   142	func (vm *VM) current() any {
   143		if len(vm.Stack) == 0 {
   144			panic("stack underflow")
   145		}
   146		return vm.Stack[len(vm.Stack)-1]
   147	}
   148	
   149	func (vm *VM) pop() any {
   150		if len(vm.Stack) == 0 {
   151			panic("stack underflow")
   152		}
   153		value := vm.Stack[len(vm.Stack)-1]
   154		vm.Stack = vm.Stack[:len(vm.Stack)-1]
   155		return value
   156	}
   157	
   158	func (vm *VM) memGrow(size uint) {
   159		vm.memory += size
   160		if vm.memory >= vm.MemoryBudget {
   161			panic("memory budget exceeded")
   162		}
   163	}
   164	
   165	func (vm *VM) scope() *Scope {
   166		return vm.Scopes[len(vm.Scopes)-1]
   167	}
   168	
   169	// allocScope returns a pointer to a Scope from the pool, growing the pool if needed.
   170	// Callers must set Len and exactly one of: Ints, Floats, Strings, Anys, or Array.
   171	func (vm *VM) allocScope() *Scope {
   172		if vm.scopePoolIdx >= len(vm.scopePool) {
   173			vm.scopePool = append(vm.scopePool, Scope{})
   174		}
   175		s := &vm.scopePool[vm.scopePoolIdx]
   176		vm.scopePoolIdx++
   177		// Reset iteration state
   178		s.Index = 0
   179		s.Count = 0
   180		s.Acc = nil

   257	func clearSlice[S ~[]E, E any](s S) {
   258		var zero E
   259		for i := range s {
   260			s[i] = zero // clear mem, optimized by the compiler, in Go 1.21 the "clear" builtin can be used

   263	
   264	// estimateFnArgsCount inspects a *Program and estimates how many function
   265	// arguments will be required to run it.
   266	func estimateFnArgsCount(program *Program) int {
   267		// Implementation note: a program will not necessarily go through all
   268		// operations, but this is just an estimation
   269		var count int
   270		for _, op := range program.Bytecode {
   271			if int(op) < len(opArgLenEstimation) {
   272				count += opArgLenEstimation[op]
   273			}
   274		}
   275		return count
   276	}
   277	
   278	var opArgLenEstimation = [...]int{
   279		OpCall1: 1,
   280		OpCall2: 2,
   281		OpCall3: 3,
   282		// we don't know exactly but we know at least 4, so be conservative as this
   283		// is only an optimization and we also want to avoid excessive preallocation
   284		OpCallN: 4,
   285		// here we don't know either, but we can guess it could be common to receive
   286		// up to 3 arguments in a function
   287		OpCallFast: 3,
   288		OpCallSafe: 3,
   289	}

   291	func (vm *VM) executeOp(program *Program, env any, op Opcode, arg int) {
   292		switch op {
   293	
   294		case OpInvalid:
   295			panic("invalid opcode")
   296	
   297		case OpPush:
   298			vm.push(program.Constants[arg])
   299	
   300		case OpInt:
   301			vm.push(arg)
   302	
   303		case OpPop:
   304			vm.pop()
   305	
   306		case OpStore:
   307			vm.Variables[arg] = vm.pop()
   308	
   309		case OpLoadVar:
   310			vm.push(vm.Variables[arg])
   311	
   312		case OpLoadConst:
   313			vm.push(runtime.Fetch(env, program.Constants[arg]))
   314	
   315		case OpLoadField:
   316			vm.push(runtime.FetchField(env, program.Constants[arg].(*runtime.Field)))
   317	
   318		case OpLoadFast:
   319			vm.push(env.(map[string]any)[program.Constants[arg].(string)])
   320	
   321		case OpLoadMethod:
   322			vm.push(runtime.FetchMethod(env, program.Constants[arg].(*runtime.Method)))
   323	
   324		case OpLoadFunc:
   325			vm.push(program.functions[arg])
   326	
   327		case OpFetch:
   328			b := vm.pop()
   329			a := vm.pop()
   330			vm.push(runtime.Fetch(a, b))
---try section---
   820			a := vm.pop()
   821			b := vm.pop()
   822			vm.push(a.(bool) && b.(bool))
   823	
   824		case OpOr:
   825			a := vm.pop()
   826			b := vm.pop()
   827			vm.push(a.(bool) || b.(bool))
   828	
   829		case OpEnd:
   830			vm.Scopes = vm.Scopes[:len(vm.Scopes)-1]
   831			if len(vm.Scopes) > 0 {
   832				vm.currScope = vm.Scopes[len(vm.Scopes)-1]
   833			} else {
   834				vm.currScope = nil
   835			}
   836	
   837		case OpTry:
   838			info := program.Constants[arg].(*TryInfo)
   839			vm.tries = append(vm.tries, tryFrame{
   840				tryIP:       vm.ip,
   841				catchIP:     info.CatchIP,
   842				finallyIP:   info.FinallyIP,
   843				stackDepth:  len(vm.Stack),
   844				scopesDepth: len(vm.Scopes),
   845				variables:   append([]any(nil), vm.Variables...),
   846				name:        info.Name,
   847				varIndex:    info.VarIndex,
   848				substr:      info.Substr,
   849			})
   850	
   851		case OpEndTry:
   852			if len(vm.tries) == 0 {
   853				panic("OpEndTry without OpTry")
   854			}
   855			vm.tries = vm.tries[:len(vm.tries)-1]
   856	
   857		case OpEndCatch:
   858			if len(vm.tries) == 0 || !vm.tries[len(vm.tries)-1].inCatch {
   859				panic("OpEndCatch without active catch")
   860			}
   861			vm.tries = vm.tries[:len(vm.tries)-1]
   862	
   863		case OpRetry:
   864			vm.retry()
   865	
   866		case OpEndFinally:
   867			// Discard the finally body result.
   868			if len(vm.Stack) > 0 {
   869				vm.pop()
   870			}
   871			if vm.pendingErr != nil {
   872				e := vm.pendingErr
   873				vm.pendingErr = nil
   874				panic(e)
   875			}
   876	
   877		default:
   878			panic(fmt.Sprintf("unknown bytecode %#x", op))
   879		}
   880	}
   881	
   882	// TryInfo holds compile-time metadata for a try/catch block.
   883	type TryInfo struct {
   884		CatchIP   int    // Absolute bytecode position of the catch handler.
   885		FinallyIP int    // Absolute bytecode position of the finally block (-1 if none).
   886		Name      string // Catch variable name (empty if not bound).
   887		VarIndex  int    // Variable index for the catch variable (-1 if not bound).
   888		Substr    string // Optional error message substring filter.
   889	}
   890	
   891	type tryFrame struct {
   892		tryIP       int
   893		catchIP     int
   894		finallyIP   int
   895		stackDepth  int
   896		scopesDepth int
   897		variables   []any
   898		retries     int
   899		inCatch     bool
   900		name        string
   901		varIndex    int
   902		substr      string
   903	}
   904	
   905	const maxRetries = 3
   906	
   907	func (vm *VM) handlePanic(r any) {
   908		err := runtime.AsError(r)
   909		message := err.Error()
   910		vm.pendingErr = nil
   911		for i := len(vm.tries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
   912			frame := &vm.tries[i]
   913			if frame.inCatch {
   914				// The catch handler itself threw. If it has a finally block,
   915				// run it before propagating the new error.
   916				if frame.finallyIP >= 0 {
   917					vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]
   918					vm.restoreTryState(frame)
   919					vm.pendingErr = err
   920					vm.ip = frame.finallyIP
   921					return
   922				}
   923				continue
   924			}
   925			if frame.substr != "" && !strings.Contains(message, frame.substr) {
   926				// The catch filter did not match. Run the finally block before
   927				// propagating the error to an outer handler.
   928				if frame.finallyIP >= 0 {
   929					vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]
   930					vm.restoreTryState(frame)
   931					vm.pendingErr = err
   932					vm.ip = frame.finallyIP
   933					return
   934				}
   935				continue
   936			}
   937			// Discard inner try frames and keep this one for the catch handler.
   938			vm.tries = vm.tries[:i+1]
   939			frame = &vm.tries[i]
   940			frame.inCatch = true
   941			vm.restoreTryState(frame)
   942			vm.push(err)
   943			vm.ip = frame.catchIP
   944			return
   945		}
   946		panic(r)
   947	}
   948	
   949	func (vm *VM) restoreTryState(frame *tryFrame) {
   950		vm.Stack = vm.Stack[:frame.stackDepth]
   951		vm.Scopes = vm.Scopes[:frame.scopesDepth]
   952		if len(vm.Scopes) > 0 {
   953			vm.currScope = vm.Scopes[len(vm.Scopes)-1]
   954		} else {
   955			vm.currScope = nil
   956		}
   957		copy(vm.Variables, frame.variables)
   958	}
   959	
   960	func (vm *VM) retry() {
   961		for i := len(vm.tries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
   962			if vm.tries[i].inCatch {
   963				frame := &vm.tries[i]
   964				if frame.retries >= maxRetries {
   965					panic(runtime.ErrRetryExhausted)
   966				}
   967				frame.retries++
   968				frame.inCatch = false
   969				vm.restoreTryState(frame)
   970				vm.ip = frame.tryIP
   971				return
   972			}
   973		}
   974		panic("retry is not allowed outside a catch block")
   975	}

STEP 153 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
The code looks clean. However, I notice a potential issue with `restoreTryState` and the `variables` snapshot: `copy(vm.Variables, frame.variables)`.
If `frame.variables` is nil (e.g., the VM's `vm.Variables` was nil at OpTry? No, it's always sized before the loop). If `vm.Variables` is longer than
`frame.variables`? They're the same length at snapshot time. Fine.

Now, let me think about a subtle bug in `handlePanic` with `vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]` when `i` is 0: `vm.tries = vm.tries[:0]` — fine, frame pointer
still valid (points into underlying array). Good.

Another subtle bug: In `handlePanic`, when `frame.inCatch` with finally, we do `vm.tries = vm.tries[:i]` and then `vm.restoreTryState(frame)`. But
`frame` is a pointer to `vm.tries[i]`. After truncating to `[:i]`, the element at index i is still in the underlying array (just beyond the slice
length), so `frame` remains valid. Good.

Now, an important concern: **`vm.tries` after `OpEndFinally` re-panic**. When OpEndFinally re-panics, the frame has already been removed (truncated in
handlePanic). So the re-panic propagates to the per-opcode recover wrapper (since len(vm.tries) might still be >0 if outer frames exist) and
handlePanic handles it. Good.

Now, another subtle issue: **The per-opcode recover wrapper uses `len(vm.tries) > 0` to decide whether to wrap**. During the catch body, the frame is
still on vm.tries (inCatch=true), so len>0 and panics in catch are wrapped. Good. During finally after normal catch completion, the frame was popped
by OpEndCatch, so len might be 0; if the finally throws, it's not wrapped by this try (correct, it propagates to outer). But if there's an outer try,
len>0 and it's wrapped for the outer. Good.

Now, let me consider **`retry` inside a finally block** — task says retry usable inside catch blocks only. If `retry()` is called in a finally block,
the frame is popped (inCatch frame gone), so vm.retry() finds no inCatch frame → panics "retry is not allowed outside a catch block". Good.

Now, let me think about **compiler: `OpStore` at catch start for named catch and the stack protocol**. On error, handlePanic restores stack to saved
depth then pushes err. The catch starts with OpStore which pops err into variable. Good. But what if the catch is the function form fallback? We emit
OpPop. Good.

Now, what about **named catch where the catch body is empty?** `try {} catch e { }` — parser requires a body; empty block fails. Fine.

Now, let me consider **`try` with `finally` but catch filter mismatch and finally throws**: handlePanic runs finally with pendingErr=original; if
finally throws, handlePanic clears pendingErr and propagates the finally error. So finally's error overrides the original (which matches spec: if
finally throws, it propagates overriding prior result). Good.

Now, let me consider **`catch` variable and `errtype` for errors that are already `*file.Error`**. If a custom user function returns a `*file.Error`
(wrapped), errtype sees its message. Fine.

Now, let me also verify the **`OpEndFinally` pop when the finally body's result is not a single value** — every expression yields one value.
SequenceNode pops intermediate. Good.

Now, let me look at the **checker's tryNode type join** — if try and catch have different types, returns any. For `try(1, "x")` type is any. For
`try(a[0], 42)` where a is []int, type is int. Good. Let me verify compile with AsInt works: `expr.Compile("try(a[0], 42)", expr.Env(env),
expr.AsInt())`. Should pass. Let me test.

	p, err := expr.Compile(`try(a[0], 42)`, expr.Env(env), expr.AsInt())

	p, err = expr.Compile(`try(a[10], "x")`, expr.Env(env), expr.AsAny())
	fmt.Println("try mixed AsAny:", err)
	if err == nil { out, _ := expr.Run(p, env); fmt.Printf("%#v\n", out) }
}

To preserve the location, I can capture `vm.ip` when the error is first recovered, and when re-panicking from OpEndFinally, temporarily set `vm.ip` to
that saved location before panicking. But `handlePanic` currently converts to `err` (an error) and discards the ip. Let me add a `pendingIP int` to
VM, set it to the ip at the time of recovery, and when OpEndFinally re-panics, set `vm.ip = vm.pendingIP` before panicking. But wait — when
re-panicking from OpEndFinally, the panic goes to the per-opcode wrapper; handlePanic may catch it (outer try) and then set vm.ip to outer catch. If
uncaught, top-level recover uses vm.ip-1, so setting vm.ip before panic gives the original location. But if an outer try catches it, handlePanic will
override vm.ip anyway. So setting `vm.ip = pendingIP` before panic is safe.

However, there's a subtlety: `vm.pendingIP` should be the ip of the instruction that threw (i.e., after the failing opcode's `vm.ip++`, so `vm.ip-1`
points to it). In handlePanic, at entry, `vm.ip` is the ip after the failing opcode. So `pendingIP = vm.ip` gives the right location via `vm.ip-1`.
Let me add.

- In handlePanic, when setting pendingErr (both inCatch and substr-mismatch cases), set `vm.pendingIP = vm.ip`.
- In OpEndFinally, before panic, set `vm.ip = vm.pendingIP` (and clear pendingIP).

 	Key   Node // Key of the pair.

 }
+
+// TryNode represents a try/catch expression.
+// It can be used in two forms:
+//
+//	try(expr, fallback)
+//
+// or:
+//
+//	try { expr } catch [name] [is "substring"] { handler } [finally { cleanup }]
+type TryNode struct {
+	base
+	Try       Node   // Expression to evaluate.
+	Catch     Node   // Handler evaluated on error.
+	Finally   Node   // Cleanup evaluated after both paths (may be nil).
+	Name      string // Optional catch variable name.
+	Substr    string // Optional substring to filter errors (ignored if empty).
+	HasSubstr bool   // Whether Substr is set.
+}
diff --git a/ast/print.go b/ast/print.go
index 1c19744..36fc7ab 100644
--- a/ast/print.go
+++ b/ast/print.go
@@ -265,3 +265,38 @@ func (n *PairNode) String() string {
 	}
 	return fmt.Sprintf("(%s): %s", n.Key.String(), n.Value.String())
 }
+
+func (n *TryNode) String() string {
+	var b strings.Builder
+	if n.Catch != nil && n.Name == "" && n.Substr == "" && n.Finally == nil {
+		// Function form: try(expr, fallback)
+		b.WriteString("try(")
+		b.WriteString(n.Try.String())
+		b.WriteString(", ")
+		b.WriteString(n.Catch.String())
+		b.WriteString(")")
+		return b.String()
+	}
+	b.WriteString("try { ")
+	b.WriteString(n.Try.String())
+	b.WriteString(" } catch")
+	if n.Name != "" {
+		b.WriteString(" ")
+		b.WriteString(n.Name)
+		if n.HasSubstr {
+			b.WriteString(" is ")
+			b.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("%q", n.Substr))
+		}
+	}
+	b.WriteString(" { ")
+	if n.Catch != nil {
+		b.WriteString(n.Catch.String())
+	}
+	b.WriteString(" }")
+	if n.Finally != nil {
+		b.WriteString(" finally { ")
+		b.WriteString(n.Finally.String())
+		b.WriteString(" }")
+	}
+	return b.String()
+}

+func ErrType(arg any) any {

+		return "none"
+	}

+	default:
+		return "custom"
+	}

+func (v *Checker) tryNode(node *ast.TryNode) Nature {
+	tryNT := v.visit(node.Try)
+
+	var catchNT Nature
+	if node.Catch != nil {
+		if node.Name != "" {
+			v.varScopes = append(v.varScopes, varScope{
+				name:   node.Name,
+				nature: v.config.NtCache.FromType(anyType),
+			})
+		}
+		catchNT = v.visit(node.Catch)
+		if node.Name != "" {
+			v.varScopes = v.varScopes[:len(v.varScopes)-1]
+		}
+	}
+
+	if node.Finally != nil {
+		_ = v.visit(node.Finally)
+	}
+
+	// A try/catch expression's type is the common type of its two branches.
+	if catchNT.Type == nil || catchNT.Type == anyType {
+		return tryNT
+	}
+	if tryNT.Type == nil || tryNT.Type == anyType {
+		return catchNT
+	}
+	if tryNT.Type == catchNT.Type {
+		return tryNT
+	}
+	return v.config.NtCache.FromType(anyType)
+}
+
 func (v *Checker) pairNode(node *ast.PairNode) Nature {
 	v.visit(node.Key)
 	v.visit(node.Value)

+func (c *compiler) TryNode(node *ast.TryNode) {
+	// Bytecode layout:
+	//   OpTry <TryInfo constant>
+	//   <try body>
+	//   OpEndTry
+	//   OpJump <finallyOrEnd>
+	//   catchIP: OpStore <varIndex> | OpPop
+	//   <catch body>
+	//   OpEndCatch
+	//   OpJump <finallyOrEnd>
+	//   finallyIP: <finally body> OpEndFinally
+	varIndex := -1
+	if node.Name != "" {
+		varIndex = c.addVariable(node.Name)
+	}
+	opTry := c.emit(OpTry, placeholder)
+
+	c.compile(node.Try)
+	c.emit(OpEndTry)
+
+	successJump := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)
+	catchIP := len(c.bytecode)
+
+	if node.Name != "" {
+		c.emit(OpStore, varIndex)
+		c.beginScope(node.Name, varIndex)
+		c.compile(node.Catch)
+		c.endScope()
+	} else {
+		c.emit(OpPop)
+		c.compile(node.Catch)
+	}
+	c.emit(OpEndCatch)
+	catchJump := c.emit(OpJump, placeholder)
+
+	finallyIP := -1
+	if node.Finally != nil {
+		finallyIP = len(c.bytecode)
+		// Both success and normal catch completion jump to the finally block.
+		c.arguments[successJump-1] = finallyIP - successJump
+		c.arguments[catchJump-1] = finallyIP - catchJump
+		c.compile(node.Finally)
+		c.emit(OpEndFinally)
+	} else {
+		c.patchJump(successJump)
+		c.patchJump(catchJump)
+	}
+
+	c.arguments[opTry-1] = c.addConstant(&TryInfo{
+		CatchIP:   catchIP,
+		FinallyIP: finallyIP,
+		Name:      node.Name,
+		VarIndex:  varIndex,
+		Substr:    node.Substr,
+	})
+}
+
 func (c *compiler) PredicateNode(node *ast.PredicateNode) {
 	c.compile(node.Node)
 }

+func (p *Parser) parseTry() Node {

package runtime

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
)

// RuntimeError is the error type produced by the throw() builtin.
// It is classified as "custom" by the errtype() builtin.
type RuntimeError struct {
	Message string
}

func (e *RuntimeError) Error() string {
	return e.Message
}

// ErrRetryExhausted is returned by retry() after the retry limit is reached.
var ErrRetryExhausted = errors.New("retry limit exceeded")

// AsError converts a recovered panic value into an error value.
func AsError(r any) error {
	if e, ok := r.(error); ok {
		return e
	}
	if s, ok := r.(string); ok {
		return errors.New(s)
	}
	return errors.New(fmt.Sprintf("%v", r))
}

# Add tests before TestNestedTry
marker='''func TestNestedTry(t *testing.T) {'''
addition='''func TestFinallyRunsOnAllPaths(t *testing.T) {
	var cleanup []string
	env := map[string]any{
		"a": []int{1, 2, 3},
		"cleanup": func(s string) any {
			cleanup = append(cleanup, s)
			return s
		},
	}

	// Finally runs after a successful try.
	cleanup = nil
	_, err := evalErr(t, `try { 1 } catch { 0 } finally { cleanup("success") }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, []string{"success"}, cleanup)

	// Finally runs after the catch handler throws.
	cleanup = nil
	_, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch { throw("catch") } finally { cleanup("catch-throw") }`, env)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "catch")
	require.Equal(t, []string{"catch-throw"}, cleanup)

	// Finally runs when the catch filter does not match.
	cleanup = nil
	_, err = evalErr(t, `try { a[10] } catch e is "nomatch" { 0 } finally { cleanup("nomatch") }`, env)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, []string{"nomatch"}, cleanup)
}

func TestErrTypeRetryExhaustion(t *testing.T) {
	env := map[string]any{
		"fail": func() any { panic("always") },
	}
	out, err := evalErr(t, `try { try { fail() } catch e { retry() } } catch e2 { errtype(e2) }`, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "retry", out)
}

'''
assert marker in s
s=s.replace(marker, addition+marker, 1)

		strings.Contains(msg, "nil map"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "from nil"),
		strings.Contains(msg, "from <nil>"):'''
assert old in s

open(p,'w').write(s)
print("done")

Now, let me also verify **`errtype` for `int("x")` and `float("x")`** — both "conversion". Done.

Now, let me consider **`throw` with a non-string value's string conversion**: `throw(42)` → message "42". Let me test `catch e is "42"` matches. Fine.

Now, let me think about **the parser's `parseTry` and the `HasSubstr` flag**. The compiler uses `node.Substr` and if `HasSubstr` is false, Substr is
empty. handlePanic checks `frame.substr != ""`. If someone writes `catch e is ""`, HasSubstr=true but Substr="" — then the filter is empty, and
`frame.substr != ""` is false, so it matches everything. That's effectively "catch any error" which is fine. But should `catch e is ""` match only
errors whose message contains ""? Every message contains "", so it matches everything. Fine.

Now, let me think about **`try` with `catch` name that's also a builtin** e.g., `catch len { }`. The parser would parse `len` as the catch name
(Identifier). Then the catch body referencing `len` would resolve to the catch variable (varScope) rather than the builtin. That's a reasonable
shadowing behavior. Fine.

Now, let me consider **`errtype` with a `*file.Error` passed from outside** (e.g., user calls `errtype(someGoError)` where someGoError is a
*file.Error). It classifies by message. Fine.

Now, let me think about whether **`try` should catch panics from `OpThrow` used internally for find/reduce errors**. Yes, OpThrow panics with an
error, and handlePanic catches it. Let me test `try { find(a, false) } catch e { errtype(e) }` — find with no match and Throws? Actually find returns

→ index. Good. Let me verify.

### Membership Operator

Fields of structs and items of maps can be accessed with `.` operator
or `[]` operator. Next two expressions are equivalent:

```expr
user.Name
user["Name"]
``` 

Elements of arrays and slices can be accessed with
`[]` operator. Negative indices are supported with `-1` being
the last element.

```expr
array[0] // first element
array[-1] // last element
```

The `in` operator can be used to check if an item is in an array or a map.

```expr

### Error Handling

Runtime errors can be caught with the `try` expression. The function form
evaluates the fallback only when the expression throws:

```expr
try(user.Name, "unknown")
```

The block form allows binding the error and filtering by message substring:

```expr
try {
    user.Name
} catch err {
    "unknown"
}
```

```expr
try {
    array[100]
} catch err is "out of range" {
    "index out of range"
}
```

An optional `finally` clause always runs after the `try`/`catch` block. If the
`finally` body throws, that error overrides the previous result:

```expr
try {
    array[100]
} catch err {
    "fallback"
} finally {
    close()
}
```

The `retry()` function can be used inside a catch block to re-execute the try
body (up to three retries):

```expr
try {
    fetch()
} catch err {
    retry()
}
```

Errors can be thrown with `throw(value)` and classified with `errtype(err)`.

### Membership Operator'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("done")
PY

# Functions

Expr comes with a set of [builtin](language-definition.md) functions, but you can also define your own functions.

The easiest way to define a custom function is to add it to the environment.

```go
env := map[string]any{
    "add": func(a, b int) int {
        return a + b
    },
}
```

Or you can use functions defined on a struct:

```go
type Env struct{}

func (Env) Add(a, b int) int {
    return a + b
}
```

:::info
If functions are marked with [`ConstExpr`](./configuration.md#constexpr) option, they will be evaluated at compile time.
:::

The best way to define a function from a performance perspective is to use a [`Function`](https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/expr-lang/expr#Function) option.

```go
atoi := expr.Function(
    "atoi",
    func(params ...any) (any, error) {
        return strconv.Atoi(params[0].(string))
    },
)

program, err := expr.Compile(`atoi("42")`, atoi)
```
89 docs/functions.md

## Error Handling

The following builtins help with error handling in combination with the
[`try` expression](language-definition.md#error-handling).

### throw(value)

Throws a custom error. The error message is the string conversion of `value`.

```expr
throw("custom error")
```

### retry()

Re-executes the `try` body. Only valid inside a `catch` block. After three
retries, a retry-exhaustion error is raised.

```expr
try {
    fetch()
} catch err {
    retry()
}
```

### errtype(err)

Classifies a caught error into one of the following strings:

| Value        | Description                                                    |
|--------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| `"index"`    | Out-of-range/bounds errors                                     |
| `"conversion"` | Type-conversion failures                                     |
| `"type"`     | Type-mismatch/assertion errors                                 |
| `"nil"`      | Nil-pointer/reference errors                                   |
| `"retry"`    | Retry-exhaustion errors                                        |
| `"custom"`   | All other errors, including those from `throw`                 |
| `"none"`     | The input is `nil`                                             |
EOF
echo done

`try then ternary` gave an error "interface conversion: interface {} is int, not bool" at the `?` — this is because `try(a[0], 0) ? "yes" : "no"` —
the ternary operator expects a bool condition, but `try(a[0], 0)` returns int 1, so the VM tries `vm.current().(bool)` and panics with interface
conversion. This is a runtime type error, not a parser issue. Actually with `expr.Eval` (no type checking), the ternary compiles and at runtime the
condition is int → panic. That's expected behavior (ternary requires bool). If I use `try(a[0], 0) == 1 ? "yes" : "no"` it would work. So no issue.
